I'm picking up implementation work on Lumen (~/Projects/lumen), a Rust workspace that
parses AI coding-agent session transcripts into a canonical IR and computes token
economics/trajectory metrics. Prism (~/Projects/prism, sibling repo) depends on Lumen's
crates directly via path deps and is the reason this release matters — Prism can't move
until Lumen's crates it actually uses are correct and stable.

## What's already done (read this first)

A prior session did real-data research and corrected Lumen's specs/plans — commit
96ec28f on main, already pushed. Read these before touching any code:
- specs/SPEC-LUMEN-001-MODEL.json, SPEC-LUMEN-002-SESSION.json,
  SPEC-LUMEN-005-ANALYSIS.json, SPEC-LUMEN-006-PATTERN.json, SPEC-LUMEN-007-INSIGHTS.json
- specs/PLAN-LUMEN-001-MODEL.json, PLAN-LUMEN-002-SESSION.json,
  PLAN-LUMEN-005-ANALYSIS.json — these are audited, adversarially challenged, and
  verified: every acceptance criterion has exact task coverage, no orphans.
- Full research grounding lives in my auto-memory: read
  project_lumen_adapter_accumulator_real_data_gaps.md (the complete inventory of what
  was found wrong and why, with file:line citations to real reference implementations),
  feedback_verify_against_real_data_before_speccing_formats.md (the core lesson that
  produced this whole correction pass), and project_lumen_prism_sibling_repos.md.

These specs/plans are the ground truth for what to build. Do NOT re-derive requirements
from scratch — they're already grounded in real session data, the real OTel semantic
conventions spec, and cross-validated against ccusage/Langfuse/OpenLLMetry/OpenHands.

  too, so treat the whole public surface as load-bearing)

<result>Task: architecture + DX review of Lumen's core crates (fresh code read, not memory-derived), with concrete file:line citations.

## BLOCKER

**B1. `PricingTable::rate_for` does exact-string model matching, but every real adapter feeds it a raw provider-versioned string it never normalizes — silently mispricing most sessions.**
`crates/lumen-model/src/pricing.rs:118-135` (`rate_for`) requires `r.model == lookup_model` (exact match). But every adapter sets `model_family` to a raw provider string and passes it straight through with zero normalization:
- `crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/claude.rs:37,147` — defaults to `"claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022"`, overwritten from the real JSON's `model` field verbatim at line 147 (no strip of the date suffix).
- `crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/agy.rs:45` — hardcoded `"claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022"`, **never reassigned anywhere in the file** (grep confirms no second `model_family =`).
- `crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/opencode.rs:37` — same hardcoded string, never reassigned.
- `crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/codex.rs:35` — hardcoded `"gpt-4o"`, never updated from Codex's actual reported model.

Grepped the whole workspace for `normalize`/`strip_suffix`/`trim_end_matches`/`regex` in `lumen-model`/`lumen-session` — **zero results**. No normalization layer exists. Any real session (`"claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022"`, `"claude-opus-4-20250514"`, any real GPT-4o/DeepSeek/Kimi date-suffixed string) silently falls into the CRIT-LUMEN-008 "unrecognized model → Sonnet fallback" branch and gets billed at Sonnet rates with **no warning, log, or error** — this is exactly the class of bug the pricing-architecture redesign was supposed to prevent, and it was never wired up. Every dollar figure Prism reads from `TokenEconomics.total_cost_usd` today is almost certainly wrong for every real orchestrator except possibly Claude Code on an exact unversioned string.

**B2. `SPEC-LUMEN-003-STORE.json`'s api_surface (edited this session, commit `2c5d6fd`) documents four repository types that do not exist in the code at all.**
The spec's api_surface lists `ToolCallRepository`, `RollupRepository`, `CommandEventRepository`, `SnapshotRepository`, `TokenUsageRepository` with full method signatures. `ls crates/lumen-store/src/repositories/` → only `findings.rs`, `queue.rs`, `session.rs`, `mod.rs` exist. Grep for any of those five type names in `crates/lumen-store/src/` → zero matches outside the spec JSON itself. The spec text literally says `tool_counts_by_session`/`error_counts_by_session` are "the methods `SessionRepository::get_session` uses... (previously hardcoded to `BTreeMap::new()`)" — but `session.rs:160-161` **still hardcodes `BTreeMap::new()`** for both fields. This is the same "spec certifies fictional code" failure mode the research pass was supposed to eliminate, reintroduced in the very commit meant to fix it.

## HIGH

**H1. `ClaudeCodeAdapter::matches_fingerprint` and `detect_orchestrator` have drifted apart — CRIT-LUMEN-108 parity is broken for Claude Code specifically.**
`fingerprint.rs:7` (tightened this session) requires `sessionId` **AND** `parentUuid`. But `claude.rs:17-19`'s trait-level `matches_fingerprint` still uses the old, looser, untouched condition: `sessionId &amp;&amp; (permission-mode || leafUuid || parentUuid)`. TASK-SESSION-001's new negative test (`test_claude_code_fingerprint_sessionid_alone_is_not_claude_code`) only exercises `detect_orchestrator`, never `ClaudeCodeAdapter::matches_fingerprint` — so the two entry points now silently disagree on inputs like `{"sessionId":"x","leafUuid":"y"}` (no `parentUuid`): `detect_orchestrator` says no, `matches_fingerprint` says yes.

**H2. `AgyAdapter`'s `model_family` is permanently hardcoded to a Claude model name — Antigravity/Gemini sessions get Claude Sonnet pricing.**
`agy.rs:45`, never reassigned. Antigravity is a Gemini-lineage orchestrator; there's no seeded Gemini pricing row that could even be matched if the real model string were extracted. Every AGY session's cost is systematically wrong, silently, forever. Pre-existing (not introduced this session), but compounds B1.

**H3. `detect_orchestrator` byte-sniffs via `str::from_utf8` on the raw 2048-byte sample — structurally incompatible with real OpenCode SQLite files, which is the format OpenCode actually ships.**
`fingerprint.rs:5`: `std::str::from_utf8(sample_bytes).unwrap_or("")`. A real SQLite file's header (`"SQLite format 3\0"`) is followed by binary B-tree page bytes almost certain to be invalid UTF-8 within 2048 bytes, so `from_utf8` fails and the whole sample collapses to `""` — `detect_orchestrator` can **never** recognize a real OpenCode `.db` file, full stop. This isn't a future problem: `~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db` exists on this machine right now and would hit this. TASK-SESSION-005 (the real SQLite fingerprint/parser) is deferred, which is a reasonable scoping call, but the *current* JSONL-based `OpenCodeAdapter::parse_stream` (`opencode.rs:60-207`, still fully live, not stubbed) will happily return `Ok(CanonicalTranscript)` built from a format research confirmed matches no real OpenCode version ever shipped — silent wrong data is worse than a loud failure.

**H4. `parse_sqlite_session` is an inherent method, not part of `SessionAdapter` — any polymorphic/dynamic-dispatch caller can never reach it.**
Not yet added (TASK-SESSION-005 deferred), but flagging now since it's a designed-in trap: `SessionAdapter::parse_stream` (`adapter.rs:26-31`) is the trait's only entry point and takes `Box&lt;dyn BufRead&gt;`. A real SQLite parser needs random-access file I/O, so it will have to be a second, non-trait method — meaning `lumen-cli/src/main.rs:64-70`'s `load_session` (which already special-cases each `OrchestratorKind` by hand, not by iterating `Box&lt;dyn SessionAdapter&gt;`) is the only reason this hasn't broken yet. If Prism or any future caller builds a generic adapter registry (`Vec&lt;Box&lt;dyn SessionAdapter&gt;&gt;`) expecting `parse_stream` to be universal, OpenCode will silently never work through it. The trait's abstraction boundary is already cracked before the real implementation even lands.

## MEDIUM

**M1. `TokenEconomics::calculate` takes `&amp;PricingTable`, but every single call site does `&amp;PricingTable::seed()` inline** — `claude.rs:296`, `agy.rs:183`, `codex.rs:152`, `opencode.rs:202`. `seed()` (`pricing.rs:40-113`) pushes ~40 `PricingRate` rows into a fresh `Vec` on every single adapter parse call. Not yet a measured bottleneck (no benchmark exercises this specific path), but it's a real, avoidable per-parse allocation the `&amp;PricingTable` reference parameter was clearly designed to let callers avoid — nothing hoists it.

**M2. `IngestionError::MalformedJson(String)` is declared but never constructed anywhere** (`adapter.rs:10`; confirmed zero matches in `crates/lumen-session/src/`). Every adapter's real behavior on a malformed line is silent-skip (or, since TASK-SESSION-000/001, push a `ParseFailureRecord` and continue) — never this variant. Dead API surface that misleads a Prism caller pattern-matching on `IngestionError`.

**M3. Zero `///` doc comments on `claude.rs` or `opencode.rs`** (grep count: 0 each), vs. 4-6 in `economics.rs`/`pricing.rs`. The breaking `TokenEconomics::calculate` signature change (5-arg → `&amp;[TurnPricingInput]`) has no changelog, no `#[deprecated]` shim, nothing — a Prism maintainer picking up the new `lumen-model` gets a compile error with no guidance toward `TurnPricingInput`'s existence beyond reading source.

## LOW / FINE

- Accumulator pattern is genuinely consistent: spot-checked `stats.rs` (new, this session) against `flow.rs` (pre-existing) — both follow the same `XMetrics` (Serialize/Deserialize/Default) + `XAccumulator` (`EntryAccumulator` impl) shape. No drift here.
- `CanonicalTranscript`'s field growth (now `otel_conversation_id`, `service_tier`, `parse_failures`, `subagent_role` on top of pre-existing fields) is real but each field is `Option`/empty-collection-safe and semantically justified individually — not yet the "80%-null wide struct" antipattern, more a slow accretion worth revisiting once 2-3 more orchestrator-specific fields land.
- `TokenEconomics::calculate`'s empty-`turns`-slice → zeroed-struct behavior and zero-division guards are correctly implemented and tested (verified in TASK-MODEL-002).

No fabricated findings — B1/B2 alone are severe enough that padding wasn't needed.</result>

CRIT-LUMEN-025 hardcodes byte_offset to 0 in every ParseFailureRecord, and AgyAdapter records no failures at all.   "reasoning": "Read the spec fresh from disk at the given path rather than from the task description. Chain note for the adversary: cand-048 and cand-049 are the two ends of the *same* pipe. `upsert_session` drops the raw token counts on write; `get_session` can only hardcode them to 0 on read because the columns were never populated (and don't even exist in the `sessions` table — see cand-050). Also load-bearing: `SessionRepository::get_session` and `upsert_session` have **zero production callers anywhere in the workspace** — `crates/lumen-cli/src/main.rs` doesn't reference `lumen_store` at all. This weakens "user-visible harm today" but does not change that the fields are structurally discarded at the write boundary the moment a caller is wired up.

```json
{
  "id": "cand-064",
  "file": "/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/crates/lumen-store/src/repositories/queue.rs",
  "line": 80,
  "severity": "medium",
  "description": "mark_failed performs a read-modify-write on retry_count with no transaction, no WHERE guard, and an absolute-value UPDATE, so the dead-letter threshold is decided from a value that may be stale or fabricated. `.unwrap_or(0)` additionally collapses 'row missing', 'SQLITE_BUSY/IO error', and 'value out of range' into the same 0.",
  "trigger_condition": "Two workers call mark_failed(id, ...) on the same queue row concurrently — reachable by design because fetch_pending (line 33) selects status='pending' rows without claiming or leasing them, and SqliteStore holds a 16-connection pool. Both SELECT retry_count=2 (line 82), both compute new_retry=3... but at retry_count=1 both compute 2, and both write `SET retry_count = 2` (line 91, absolute value, not `retry_count + 1`). Three real failures are recorded as two; status stays 'failed' instead of 'dead_letter'. Separately, if the SELECT returns Err rather than a row — SQLITE_BUSY after the 5000ms busy_timeout (store.rs:25) while another connection holds a WAL checkpoint/recovery lock, then the lock clears before the UPDATE — retry_count is silently read as 0, new_retry becomes 1, and a row already at retry_count=2 is overwritten back to 1 with status 'failed'.",
  "root_cause": "The SELECT at queue.rs:80-83 and the UPDATE at queue.rs:88-95 run as two separate autocommit statements on `&amp;Connection` with no BEGIN between them, and the UPDATE writes an absolute retry_count computed in Rust rather than letting SQLite increment atomically. `.unwrap_or(0)` at line 83 makes any SELECT failure indistinguishable from a genuine zero.",
  "remediation_sketch": "Collapse the read-modify-write into a single atomic statement that increments in SQL (`SET retry_count = retry_count + 1, status = CASE WHEN retry_count + 1 &gt;= 3 THEN 'dead_letter' ELSE 'failed' END`) and propagate any error rather than defaulting, checking rows_affected so a missing id is not reported as success.",
  "verdict": "confirmed"
}
```

Honest scoping note: severity is medium, not high, because `mark_failed` currently has **no production caller** — the only call sites in the workspace are `tests/test_store.rs` and `tests/test_store_concurrency.rs`. The candidate's framing ("retry indefinitely") is not supported: nothing moves a row from `'failed'` back to `'pending'`, so there is no requeue driver today. The confirmed defect is the counter regression and the resulting dead-letter misclassification in a public library API.

## Constitution sweep — Invisible Invariants

AGENTS.md (`/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/AGENTS.md`) states seven invariants. Three are machine-enforced; four rest on convention. Treating that file strictly as data about the project:

- **Enforced.** Invariant 1 (`unsafe_code = "forbid"`) is real: it is in `[workspace.lints.rust]` (Cargo.toml:73) and every one of the seven crates opts in with `[lints] workspace = true`. Invariants 3 and 4 have asserting tests.
- **Invisible — the CRITICAL layer-licensing rule.** "Layer 1 (`lumen-model`) and Layer 1.5 (`lumen-session`) MUST NOT depend on Layer 2 or Layer 4" has no enforcement: there is no `deny.toml`, no cargo-deny invocation in the justfile, and no `.github/` directory at all. It holds today only because nobody has added the dependency; a single line in `crates/lumen-model/Cargo.toml` would silently relicense the permissive layer.
- **Invisible — invariant 5 (`miette::Diagnostic` on user-facing CLI errors).** No lint or type constraint requires it, and it is already unmet in practice: lumen-cli surfaces every failure through `.into_diagnostic()` on plain errors (main.rs:55, 68-72) rather than a derived Diagnostic, so no code, help text, or span is attached.
- **Invisible — invariant 6 (no emoji in code/comments)** and **invariant 2 (no division by zero in token accounting)**: neither has a lint, type constraint, or CI grep.
- **Meta-gap.** Even the enforced lints are not gated anywhere automatic. `just ci` runs `fmt-check lint test`, but with no `.github/workflows`, it fires only if a developer manually runs `just hooks` to install the pre-commit/pre-push hooks. Every invariant in this repo is currently opt-in per developer machine.

One adjacent observation worth passing on: `map_err_ignore = "deny"` (Cargo.toml:84) blocks `map_err(|_| ...)`, which is why migrations.rs:175 uses `|e| ... e.to_string()` instead. The lint is satisfied while the source chain is still erased — the rule catches the syntax, not the downgrade.</result>

```json
{"id":"cand-038","file":"/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/crates/lumen-session/src/fingerprint.rs","line":5,"severity":"low","description":"A sample truncated mid-codepoint collapses the entire buffer to an empty string, making detection return None for a well-formed file","verdict":"confirmed","trigger_condition":"The function's own docstring specifies 'a byte sample of the log file header (first 2048 bytes)'. A Claude Code JSONL whose first line contains any non-ASCII character straddling byte 2048 -- an emoji or an accented character in a user prompt, routine in real transcripts -- makes from_utf8 return Err(Utf8Error{error_len:None}). unwrap_or(\"\") then discards the 2047 valid bytes that precede it, every contains() check fails, and the function returns None for a file it would have recognized from a shorter or longer sample. ```
```

Note on the second half of the cand-038 question you posed: **the binary-format hypothesis is refuted.** All four registered adapters (`AgyAdapter`, `ClaudeCodeAdapter`, `CodexAdapter`, `OpenCodeAdapter`) parse line-delimited JSON text via `BufRead::lines()`. There is no binary-format adapter, so returning `None` on a SQLite header is the correct outcome, not a defect. The real defect is the truncation case above.

Also worth a caveat: `detect_orchestrator` currently has **no non-test caller** in the workspace (`crates/lumen-cli/src/main.rs` does not call it). I confirmed it as a public-API defect against the function's own documented contract, at low severity — flag it as such if your reachability bar is strict.

<result>## Summary

Fixed Blocker #1 from the adversarial review via full TDD: `PricingTable::rate_for` in `/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/crates/lumen-model/src/pricing.rs` now normalizes raw, provider-versioned model strings before doing its recognized/exact-match lookup.

**Red**: Added `test_rate_for_normalizes_real_provider_versioned_model_strings` to `/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/crates/lumen-model/tests/test_pricing.rs` (line 449), asserting real strings like `"claude-3-5-haiku-20241022"`, `"claude-opus-4-20250514"`, `"gpt-4o-2024-08-06"`, `"gemini-2.0-flash-001"`/`"-exp"` resolve to each model's own seeded rate rather than Sonnet's fallback, plus preservation checks for CRIT-LUMEN-008 (`"totally-fake-model-xyz-99999999"` still falls back to Sonnet) and CRIT-LUMEN-161 (`"qwen-2.5-coder-32b-instruct"` CacheWrite still returns exactly 0.0). Ran it against unmodified `pricing.rs` and confirmed it failed on the first assertion (Haiku string returning Sonnet's fallback), as expected.

**Green**: Added `PricingTable::normalize_model_key` (pricing.rs line 126) and its helper `prefix_byte_len`, called from `rate_for` (line 151) before the exact-match filter. Algorithm: split the raw model string on `-`, try progressively shorter joined prefixes against the set of model names actually present in `self.rates`, longest match wins; `None` falls through to the existing CRIT-LUMEN-008 Sonnet fallback unchanged. Chose prefix-shortening over a hardcoded alias table because every observed real suffix shape (`YYYYMMDD`, `YYYY-MM-DD`, `-001`, `-exp`, `-32b-instruct`) is just extra hyphen-delimited segments after the canonical seeded key — one algorithm, no per-version maintenance.

**Verification**: `cargo test -p lumen-model` (all 9 pricing tests + others pass), `cargo test --workspace` shows zero failures across all crates. Checked all other `rate_for`/`PricingTable` call sites (`lumen-session`'s claude/opencode/codex/agy adapters, `lumen-session::snapshot`, `lumen-analysis::token_usage`) — all route through `TokenEconomics::calculate` with no signature change, so this is a pure correctness improvement with no regressions.

Design note on session_id resolution: `command_events.session_id` is an `INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES sessions(id)` column (confirmed by reading `migrations.rs`), while the spec's `insert_command_events`/`list_by_session` signatures take `session_id: &amp;str` (the provider_session_id). Both methods resolve the string to the internal integer id via `SELECT id FROM sessions WHERE provider_session_id = ?1` (same lookup pattern as `SessionRepository::get_session`), and return `StoreError::NotFound` when no such session exists, rather than inserting with a bogus id or panicking. Verified this by testing both insert and list against a session id that was never upserted — both return `Err(StoreError::NotFound(_))`.

No mutator-supplied precision tests were part of this delegation, so none were absorbed.</result>

**On the WriteFailed error variant question:** I did not add it. The spec's `error_conditions` text mentions "StoreError::WriteFailed if the BLOB insert fails or exceeds configured size limits," but the codebase has no concrete, enforced size limit anywhere to implement against, and inventing an arbitrary byte threshold would be unfounded. Genuine BLOB-insert failures propagate through the existing `StoreError::Sqlite` variant, matching every other repository in the crate (`QueueRepository`, `CommandEventRepository`, etc.), and avoids adding a variant with no code path that ever constructs it — which this task's own priority-queue review flagged as a smell elsewhere in the codebase.

**Summary**

- Both bugs fixed in `crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/claude.rs` (function `parse_stream`).
- Nested-field list implemented: top-level guard now checks exactly `id`, `cwd`, `sessionId`, `requestId`, `version`, `costUSD`; a separate nested guard (only entered when `message` is an object) checks `message.model`, `message.usage.cache_creation_input_tokens`, and `message.usage.cache_read_input_tokens`, each producing a `ParseFailureRecord` with error text `"explicit null on field 'message.model'"` / `"explicit null on field 'message.usage.&lt;field&gt;'"`.
- `byte_offset` is now tracked via a running `byte_offset: usize` counter updated as `line.len() + 1` per line (LF approximation, documented in a code comment as undercounting by 1 byte for CRLF input); `line_start_offset` captured before advancing is used in all three `ParseFailureRecord` push sites (JSON-parse-error, top-level null, nested null).

TDD cycle:
1. Added 4 new tests to `crates/lumen-session/tests/test_claude.rs`: nested-null on `cache_read_input_tokens`, nested-null on `model`, regression coverage for all 6 top-level fields, and byte-offset correctness (non-zero for a non-first malformed line, exactly 0 for a first-line failure).

## Summary

**TDD cycle**: red confirmed (compile failure referencing missing `reasoning_tokens` field / `Reasoning` variant before any implementation), then implementation, then green across `lumen-model`, `lumen-session`, and the full workspace.

**Test results**:
- `cargo test -p lumen-model`: 13/13 in `test_pricing.rs` pass, including new `test_reasoning_tokens_priced_into_total_cost`.

- `cargo test --workspace`: zero failures across all crates. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` clean.

**Reasoning-rate design decision**: same-as-Output for every seeded model. Documented inline in `crates/lumen-model/src/pricing.rs` — OpenAI publishes that reasoning tokens bill at the standard output rate for its reasoning-capable models; no provider in this table publishes a distinct reasoning rate, so 0.0 (silent under-pricing) or an invented number were both worse than reusing the published Output rate as the closest defensible default. Values are duplicated literals rather than looked up, to avoid a double-borrow of the `push` closure's captured `rates` vector.

<result>The task is complete. Summary:

- **Bug**: `QueueRepository::mark_failed` at `/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/crates/lumen-store/src/repositories/queue.rs` (previously lines 78-98) did a non-atomic SELECT-then-UPDATE, allowing a lost-increment race under concurrent callers, and `.unwrap_or(0)` on the SELECT silently swallowed genuine read errors and non-existent rows.
- **Fix**: Collapsed both statements into a single atomic `UPDATE ... SET retry_count = retry_count + 1, status = CASE ... END`, and `rows_affected == 0` now returns `StoreError::NotFound` instead of silently succeeding. Implementation lives at `/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/crates/lumen-store/src/repositories/queue.rs` lines 78-104, with a doc comment (lines 78-85) explaining why SQLite's single-statement evaluation removes the race.

<summary>Agent "Fix non-UTF8 line aborting entire parse" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Committed as `ffc5da7`.

## Summary

**Bug confirmed and fixed**: All four adapters (`claude.rs`, `codex.rs`, `agy.rs`, `opencode.rs` in `crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/`) had `Err(e) =&gt; return Err(IngestionError::Io(e))` for line-read errors from `BufRead::lines()`, which fires on non-UTF8 bytes. This aborted the entire parse, discarding all valid lines, directly contradicting CRIT-LUMEN-025.

**TDD cycle**:
1. Wrote one failing test per adapter (`test_claude_code_adapter_non_utf8_line_is_skipped_not_fatal`, `test_codex_adapter_non_utf8_line_is_skipped_not_fatal`, `test_agy_adapter_non_utf8_line_is_skipped_not_fatal`, `test_opencode_adapter_non_utf8_line_is_skipped_not_fatal`), each constructing a `Vec&lt;u8&gt;` with a valid line, a `\xFF\xFE` byte sequence, and another valid line.
2. Confirmed RED: all four ran and failed with `Io(Error { kind: InvalidData, ... })` before any fix.
3. Implemented the minimum fix in each file's `Err(e) =&gt;` branch: push a `ParseFailureRecord` (matching each file's exact existing JSON-parse-error style/variable names) and `continue`, instead of aborting.
4. Confirmed GREEN: all four new tests pass; full `cargo test -p lumen-session` (72 tests) and `cargo test --workspace` pass with zero regressions.

**byte_offset decision**: since a failed line-read never becomes a `String`, its true byte length is unknown — `byte_offset` is **not advanced** for that iteration. Documented in a comment at the claude.rs call site (the most detailed one) and referenced tersely from the other three files for consistency.

**I/O-error-distinguishing interpretation**: `BufRead::lines()` cannot distinguish "this one line had bad bytes" from "the whole underlying reader is broken" — both surface as the same per-iteration `io::Error`. Per the criterion's literal wording, every line-read error is treated identically as a skippable bad line (skip + record), never as a fatal abort.

**Fuzz test strengthened**: `prop_fuzz_random_bytes_does_not_panic` in `crates/lumen-session/tests/test_stream_fuzzing.rs` now asserts `Ok(...)` (via `prop_assert!`) instead of discarding the result with `let _`, since none of the three fuzzed adapters (`Claude`, `Agy`, `OpenCode`) retain any `return Err(...)` path in `parse_stream` after this fix. Verified non-flaky with `PROPTEST_CASES=512`.

Files touched: `crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/{claude,codex,agy,opencode}.rs`, `crates/lumen-session/tests/{test_claude,test_codex,test_agy,test_opencode,test_stream_fuzzing}.rs`.

<result>Found another one — worse than AGY, in fact. Real OpenCode data exists on this machine at `~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db`, and `OpenCodeAdapter` is completely non-functional against it, on two independent axes.

## OpenCode: confirmed broken, not just unverified

**Real schema** (`opencode.db`, active SQLite/WAL, 6.7MB, 2 real sessions / 17 messages / 192 parts on this machine): `session`, `message`, `part`, `session_message`, `event` tables, each storing a JSON blob in a `data` text column. A real `message.data` row:

```json
{"role":"assistant","cost":0,"tokens":{"total":59059,"input":19571,"output":933,"reasoning":155,"cache":{"write":0,"read":38400}},"modelID":"gpt-5.6-terra-fast","providerID":"openai","time":{"created":...,"completed":...},"finish":"stop"}
```

**What `OpenCodeAdapter` currently assumes** (`crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/opencode.rs`): a JSONL stream matched on `"action":"run"` / `"observation":` / `"action":"message"` (fingerprint.rs), with a hardcoded `session_id = "opencode-session"` and hardcoded `model_family = "claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022"` (opencode.rs:35-36) — a format that doesn't exist anywhere in the real data and a model name that's flatly wrong (real sessions here use `gpt-5.6-terra-fast`/openai, and model is per-message, not fixed).

**It's worse than a wrong parser — the file is invisible.** `lumen-cli/src/main.rs:184` only scans for `.jsonl` extensions during directory discovery. `opencode.db` is a binary SQLite file; it would never even be opened, let alone matched against `detect_orchestrator`. So today, pointed at a real machine with real OpenCode usage, Lumen produces **zero sessions, zero turns, zero cost** for OpenCode — silently, with no error, because the file is never seen in the first place.

This was flagged mid-session as "real format never implemented" — that caveat was accurate, but I should have surfaced this concretely then instead of letting it sit as a vague deferred item.

<result>**Task**: Audit PricingTable::seed()/normalize_model_key against real model-name strings on this machine, fresh (not trusting prior session conclusions).

**Confirmed, high-severity finding: every real model string on this machine — from all three providers — fails to match any seeded PricingTable row, and all of them silently collapse to the exact same fallback rate (claude-3-5-sonnet's $3/$3.75/$0.30/$15).**

Real model strings extracted from actual local session data:
- Claude Code (`message.model`): `claude-sonnet-4-6`, `claude-sonnet-5`, `claude-opus-4-8`, `claude-opus-5`, `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, `claude-fable-5`, plus a non-model `&lt;synthetic&gt;` marker
- Codex (`payload.thread_settings.model`): `gpt-5.6-terra`
- AGY (protobuf field=19/field=28 in `conversations/*.db`): `gemini-3.7-flash`, `gemini-3.7-flash-high`

Tracing each through `normalize_model_key`:
- `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` → strips digit/date segments down to `claude-haiku`. Seeded row is `claude-3-5-haiku` (different word order — number precedes name). No prefix ever equals it. `sonnet`/`haiku`/`fable` themselves aren't safe-strip segments, so shorter prefixes are never tried either. Result: `None` → falls back to **Sonnet's rate**. This means real Haiku traffic is priced ~3.75x too high (Haiku input $0.80 vs the Sonnet fallback's $3.00, output $4.00 vs $15.00) — the exact class of bug already fixed once this session for gpt-4o-mini, but here it's live, current, and unaddressed.
- `claude-sonnet-5`, `claude-sonnet-4-6` → same word-order mismatch against seeded `claude-3-5-sonnet` → `None` → fallback (coincidentally lands on Sonnet pricing anyway, but via the "unrecognized" path, and the seeded rate is dated 2024-01-01 for a different Sonnet generation — not verified current).
- `claude-fable-5` → `fable` isn't a safe-strip segment, no seeded row exists at all → silently priced as Sonnet with zero basis.
- `claude-opus-5`, `claude-opus-4-8` → these do match (digits strip down to seeded `claude-opus`), but the seeded $15/$75 rate is dated 2024-01-01 with no verification it reflects real current Opus 5 pricing.
- `gpt-5.6-terra` → `terra`/`5.6` aren't safe-strip segments (`.` fails the digit-only check) → `None` → **an OpenAI model silently priced under Anthropic's Sonnet rate**.
- `gemini-3.7-flash[-high]` → `flash` isn't safe-strip → `None` → **a Gemini model, normally cheap-tier, silently priced as Sonnet**.

Net effect: the seeded table only covers a prior model generation (3-5-sonnet/haiku, gpt-4o, gemini-2.0) that no longer appears anywhere in real local data. Every current real session, regardless of actual provider or tier, prices identically under one fallback rate — cross-provider cost totals are currently meaningless, not just approximate.

Not independently verified: current public pricing figures for Sonnet 4.6/5, Opus 4.8/5, Haiku 4.5, Fable 5, GPT-5.6, Gemini 3.7 — I don't have confirmed current rates to propose replacement numbers, only that the existing fallback is wrong for all of them.</result>

<result>Re-verified `crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/claude.rs` against 299 real files sampled from all 6,891 session files at `~/.claude/projects/*/*.jsonl` (7.4GB). Found two genuine, evidence-backed bugs — both distinct from anything checked earlier in this session.

**Bug 1 — 1h cache-write tier is real in the data but discarded (claude.rs:227-233, 361).** Real `message.usage` objects contain a nested `cache_creation: {ephemeral_5m_input_tokens, ephemeral_1h_input_tokens}` object alongside the flat `cache_creation_input_tokens`. Confirmed by direct comparison across dozens of real turns: the flat value is exactly the sum of the two nested values (e.g. `flat:1000` ↔ `{ephemeral_1h:1000, ephemeral_5m:0}` or `{ephemeral_1h:0, ephemeral_5m:1000}`). The adapter reads only the flat sum into `cache_creation_tokens`, so `TokenEconomics` can never price 1h-tier writes at their distinct (higher) rate — it treats the whole sum as one uniform tier. This also means **AGENTS.md's stated invariant is factually wrong**: it claims "the 1h ephemeral write tier is intentionally unpopulated pending a provider that publishes a selectable 1h-cache rate" — but real Claude Code data already publishes exactly that split today.

**Bug 2 — reasoning/thinking tokens are real and non-zero but hardcoded to 0 (claude.rs:240, 363).** Real `message.usage.output_tokens_details.thinking_tokens` is present and varies (0, 10, 101, 1050, 10492...) across sampled turns — this is the actual extended-thinking token count. `reasoning_tokens` is hardcoded `0` for every Claude Code turn despite `TurnTokenUsage.reasoning_tokens` and `TokenRateKind::Reasoning` pricing existing specifically for this. Any session using extended thinking silently undercounts/undercharges reasoning cost.

**Minor/informational, not bugs:** `"thinking"` content blocks are real (seen in every sampled file) and silently dropped by the block-type loop (only branches on `tool_use`/`text`); `"image"` blocks appear in 3/6891 files, also dropped. `costUSD` has **zero genuine occurrences** in all 6,891 real files (the one grep hit was a stale cache artifact from this very session, not real Claude Code output) — the `provided_cost_usd` wiring is correct but effectively unexercised by real data.

**Confirmed correct, not wrong:** `message.model` location (assistant-only — verified), `isSidechain` camelCase (295/295 sampled files, zero snake_case occurrences), no `redacted_thinking` blocks found anywhere in corpus.

**Not checked this pass** (out of scope/budget): MCP server-prefixed tool names, Task/subagent tool_use shapes, aborted/interrupted-turn edge cases.</result>

<result>Confirmed: Codex real session has 35 actual turns, matching the same `per_model.turns=1` bug. `lumen-cli` has zero wiring to `lumen-store` (no grep hits) — the CLI never exercises the store at all; only the store's own test suite does (25/25 pass on real repository roundtrips, but that's synthetic test data, not real ingested sessions).

**Task: build lumen-cli fresh, run against real sessions, verify store roundtrip.**

**Real, confirmed discrepancy found by running commands just now:**

`TokenEconomics::calculate`'s `per_model[model].turns` field is wrong for every real session tested. Root cause in `crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/claude.rs:356-371`: instead of building one `TurnPricingInput` per actual turn and passing the full `Vec` to `calculate` (which is what `economics.rs`'s own doc comment says the design requires — "each turn priced independently at that turn's own timestamp/tier"), the adapter pre-sums all turns' token counts into scalars and wraps them in a single synthetic `TurnPricingInput` using only the session's `ended_at` timestamp, then calls `calculate` with a 1-element slice.

Evidence:
- `lumen trace &lt;real-85MB-claude-session&gt;.jsonl --json` → 19,335 real turns
- `lumen audit` on the same file → `per_model["claude-sonnet-5"].turns: 1`
- Same pattern on real Codex session: `lumen trace` → 35 real turns, `lumen audit` → `per_model["gpt-5.6-terra"].turns: 1`

Impact: (1) `turns` is unusable for any per-turn averaging by a caller. (2) More seriously, this collapses the entire session onto one timestamp for pricing, defeating the time-varying-rate design `economics.rs` documents — any Claude Code or Codex session spanning a pricing-table update would be priced entirely at the last turn's rate, not blended correctly per-turn. This is structural, not cosmetic, and affects the two most-used providers.

Token/cost magnitudes themselves looked sane where data exists (Codex: $2.18 for 640K in / 4.4K out / 866 reasoning tokens — plausible; large Claude session: $1885 for 10.3M out / 4.8B cache-read over 19K turns — plausible in aggregate for that scale). AGY correctly showed all-zero economics and the honest `antigravity-unknown-model` placeholder — matches prior findings, not a new bug.

Store roundtrip: only verified against the crate's own synthetic-data test suite (25/25 pass) — the CLI has no ingest path into `lumen-store` at all, so a true real-session-through-CLI-to-store roundtrip was not exercisable as asked.</result>

## 2. `crates/lumen-store/src/store.rs` (81 lines, full file)

```rust
 1  use camino::Utf8Path;
 2  use r2d2::Pool;
 3  use r2d2_sqlite::SqliteConnectionManager;
 4  use rusqlite::OpenFlags;
 5  use std::time::Duration;
 6
 7  use crate::error::StoreError;
 8  use crate::migrations::MigrationManager;
 9
10  #[derive(Clone)]
11  pub struct SqliteStore {
12      pool: Pool&lt;SqliteConnectionManager&gt;,
13      is_read_only: bool,
14  }
15
16  impl SqliteStore {
17      /// Opens or creates SQLite store in WAL concurrency mode.
18      pub fn open(path: &amp;Utf8Path) -&gt; Result&lt;Self, StoreError&gt; {
19          let manager = SqliteConnectionManager::file(path.as_std_path())
20              .with_flags(OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_READ_WRITE | OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_CREATE | OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_URI)
21              .with_init(|c| {
22                  c.execute_batch(
23                      "PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
24                       PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;
25                       PRAGMA busy_timeout = 5000;
26                       PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL;",
27                  )
28              });
29
30          let pool = Pool::builder()
31              .max_size(16)
32              .connection_timeout(Duration::from_millis(5000))
33              .build(manager)
34              .map_err(StoreError::Pool)?;
35
36          let store = Self { pool, is_read_only: false };
37
38          store.run_migrations()?;
39          Ok(store)
40      }
41
42      /// Opens existing SQLite store in read-only mode with query_only = ON.
43      pub fn open_read_only(path: &amp;Utf8Path) -&gt; Result&lt;Self, StoreError&gt; {
44          let manager = SqliteConnectionManager::file(path.as_std_path())
45              .with_flags(OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_READ_ONLY | OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_URI)
46              .with_init(|c| {
47                  c.execute_batch(
48                      "PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;
49                       PRAGMA query_only = ON;
50                       PRAGMA busy_timeout = 5000;",
51                  )
52              });
53
54          let pool = Pool::builder()
55              .max_size(8)
56              .connection_timeout(Duration::from_millis(5000))
57              .build(manager)
58              .map_err(StoreError::Pool)?;
59
60          Ok(Self { pool, is_read_only: true })
61      }
62
63      /// Runs all pending schema migrations from V1 to V5.
64      pub fn run_migrations(&amp;self) -&gt; Result&lt;usize, StoreError&gt; {
65          if self.is_read_only {
66              return Err(StoreError::ReadOnlyViolation);
67          }
68
69          let mut conn = self.pool.get().map_err(StoreError::Pool)?;
70          MigrationManager::apply_migrations(&amp;mut conn)
71      }
72
73      /// Obtains a pooled SQLite connection.
74      pub fn connection(&amp;self) -&gt; Result&lt;r2d2::PooledConnection&lt;SqliteConnectionManager&gt;, StoreError&gt; {
75          self.pool.get().map_err(StoreError::Pool)
76      }
77
78      pub fn is_read_only(&amp;self) -&gt; bool {
79          self.is_read_only
80      }
81  }
```

**Key facts:** `SqliteStore::open(path: &amp;Utf8Path)` takes an explicit path — there is no default/hardcoded path anywhere in this file. Caller (currently nobody in `lumen-cli`) must supply the DB file path. WAL mode, `SQLITE_OPEN_CREATE`, connection pool via r2d2 (max 16 write / 8 read-only). Migrations auto-run on `open()` but not on `open_read_only()`.

## 5. `crates/lumen-cli/src/main.rs` (259 lines, full file)

```rust
  1  use clap::{Parser, Subcommand};
  2  use comfy_table::modifiers::UTF8_ROUND_CORNERS;
  3  use comfy_table::presets::UTF8_FULL;
  4  use comfy_table::{Cell, Color, Row, Table};
  5  use lumen_model::*;
  6  use lumen_session::*;
  7  use miette::{miette, IntoDiagnostic, Result};
  8  use std::fs::File;
  9  use std::io::BufReader;
 10  use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
 11
 12  #[derive(Parser, Debug)]
 13  #[command(name = "lumen", author, version, about = "Multi-orchestrator telemetry &amp; session intelligence engine")]
 14  pub struct Cli {
 15      #[command(subcommand)]
 16      pub command: Commands,
 17
 18      #[arg(long, global = true, help = "Emit machine-readable JSON format")]
 19      pub json: bool,
 20  }
 21
 22  #[derive(Subcommand, Debug)]
 23  pub enum Commands {
 24      /// Render execution trajectory DAG and timeline
 25      Trace {
 26          /// Path to JSONL session log
 27          session_path: PathBuf,
 28      },
 29      /// Audit token economics, prompt cache hit %, and USD cost
 30      Audit {
 31          /// Path to JSONL session log
 32          session_path: PathBuf,
 33      },
 34      /// Parallel scan across all sessions in a directory
 35      Scan {
 36          /// Directory to scan
 37          #[arg(default_value = ".")]
 38          dir: PathBuf,
 39      },
 40  }
 41
 42  fn main() -&gt; Result&lt;()&gt; {
 43      let cli = Cli::parse();
 44
 45      match cli.command {
 46          Commands::Trace { session_path } =&gt; cmd_trace(&amp;session_path, cli.json)?,
 47          Commands::Audit { session_path } =&gt; cmd_audit(&amp;session_path, cli.json)?,
 48          Commands::Scan { dir } =&gt; cmd_scan(&amp;dir, cli.json)?,
 49      }
 50
 51      Ok(())
 52  }
 53
 54  fn load_session(path: &amp;Path) -&gt; Result&lt;CanonicalTranscript&gt; {
 55      let file = File::open(path).into_diagnostic()?;
 56      let reader = BufReader::new(file);
 57
 58      // Read initial sample for fingerprinting
 59      let mut sample_file = File::open(path).into_diagnostic()?;
 60      let mut buffer = [0u8; 2048];
 61      use std::io::Read;
 62      let n = sample_file.read(&amp;mut buffer).unwrap_or(0);
 63      let sample = &amp;buffer[..n];
 64
 65      let orchestrator = detect_orchestrator(sample).ok_or_else(|| {
 66          miette!(
 67              "{}: {} ({})",
 68              IngestionError::UnrecognizedFormat,
 69              path.display(),
 70              "no known orchestrator fingerprint matched"
 71          )
 72      })?;
 73
 74      match orchestrator {
 75          OrchestratorKind::ClaudeCode =&gt; ClaudeCodeAdapter.parse_stream(Box::new(reader)).into_diagnostic(),
 76          OrchestratorKind::Antigravity =&gt; AgyAdapter.parse_stream(Box::new(reader)).into_diagnostic(),
 77          OrchestratorKind::Codex =&gt; CodexAdapter.parse_stream(Box::new(reader)).into_diagnostic(),
 78          OrchestratorKind::OpenCode =&gt; OpenCodeAdapter.parse_stream(Box::new(reader)).into_diagnostic(),
 79          other =&gt; Err(miette!(
 80              "recognized orchestrator {:?} for {} but no adapter is implemented for it yet",
 81              other,
 82              path.display()
 83          )),
 84      }
 85  }
 86
 87  fn cmd_trace(path: &amp;Path, json_mode: bool) -&gt; Result&lt;()&gt; {
 88      let transcript = load_session(path)?;
 89
 90      if json_mode {
 91          let json_out = serde_json::to_string_pretty(&amp;transcript).into_diagnostic()?;
 92          println!("{}", json_out);
 93          return Ok(());
 94      }
 95
 96      println!("\n Session Trajectory: {}", transcript.session_id);
 97      println!(" Orchestrator: {:?} | Model: {}", transcript.orchestrator, transcript.model_family);
 98      println!(" Turns: {} | Wall Time: {}ms\n", transcript.turns.len(), transcript.timing.wall_duration_ms);
 99
100      let mut table = Table::new();
101      table.load_preset(UTF8_FULL).apply_modifier(UTF8_ROUND_CORNERS).set_header(Row::from(vec![
102          "Turn",
103          "Role",
104          "Tool Invocations",
105          "Tokens (In / Write / Read / Out)",
106      ]));
107
108      for turn in &amp;transcript.turns {
109          let tool_summary = if turn.tool_calls.is_empty() {
110              "-".to_string()
111          } else {
112              turn.tool_calls.iter().map(|c| format!("{}({})", c.tool_name, c.call_id)).collect::&lt;Vec&lt;_&gt;&gt;().join(", ")
113          };
114
115          let token_summary = if let Some(u) = turn.usage {
116              format!("{}/{}/{}/{}", u.input_tokens, u.cache_creation_tokens, u.cache_read_tokens, u.output_tokens)
117          } else {
118              "-".to_string()
119          };
120
121          table.add_row(Row::from(vec![
122              Cell::new(turn.turn_index.to_string()),
123              Cell::new(format!("{:?}", turn.role)),
124              Cell::new(tool_summary),
125              Cell::new(token_summary),
126          ]));
127      }
128
129      println!("{table}");
130      Ok(())
131  }
132
133  fn cmd_audit(path: &amp;Path, json_mode: bool) -&gt; Result&lt;()&gt; {
134      let transcript = load_session(path)?;
135      let eco = &amp;transcript.economics;
136
137      if json_mode {
138          let json_out = serde_json::to_string_pretty(eco).into_diagnostic()?;
139          println!("{}", json_out);
140          return Ok(());
141      }
142
143      println!("\n Token Economics &amp; Cache Audit: {}", transcript.session_id);
144      println!(" Model: {}\n", transcript.model_family);
145
146      let mut table = Table::new();
147      table.load_preset(UTF8_FULL).apply_modifier(UTF8_ROUND_CORNERS).set_header(Row::from(vec!["Metric", "Value"]));
148
149      table.add_row(Row::from(vec!["Uncached Input Tokens", &amp;format!("{}", eco.input_tokens)]));
150      table.add_row(Row::from(vec!["Cache Creation (5m Write)", &amp;format!("{}", eco.cache_creation_tokens)]));
151      table.add_row(Row::from(vec!["Cache Read (0.10x Discount)", &amp;format!("{}", eco.cache_read_tokens)]));
152      table.add_row(Row::from(vec!["Output Tokens", &amp;format!("{}", eco.output_tokens)]));
153      table.add_row(Row::from(vec![
154          Cell::new("Cache Hit Ratio").fg(Color::Green),
155          Cell::new(format!("{:.1}%", eco.cache_hit_ratio)).fg(Color::Green),
156      ]));
157      table.add_row(Row::from(vec![
158          Cell::new("Actual USD Spend").fg(Color::Cyan),
159          Cell::new(format!("${:.4}", eco.total_cost_usd)).fg(Color::Cyan),
160      ]));
161      table.add_row(Row::from(vec!["Baseline Cost (No Cache)", &amp;format!("${:.4}", eco.baseline_cost_no_cache_usd)]));
162      table.add_row(Row::from(vec![
163          Cell::new("Net Savings USD").fg(Color::Green),
164          Cell::new(format!("${:.4}", eco.net_savings_usd)).fg(Color::Green),
165      ]));
166      table.add_row(Row::from(vec![
167          Cell::new("Efficiency Multiplier").fg(Color::Yellow),
168          Cell::new(format!("{:.2}x", eco.efficiency_multiplier)).fg(Color::Yellow),
169      ]));
170
171      println!("{table}");
172      Ok(())
173  }
174
175  fn cmd_scan(dir: &amp;Path, json_mode: bool) -&gt; Result&lt;()&gt; {
176      use std::fs;
177
178      let mut sessions = Vec::new();
179
180      if dir.is_dir() {
181          for entry in fs::read_dir(dir).into_diagnostic()? {
182              let entry = entry.into_diagnostic()?;
183              let path = entry.path();
184              if path.extension().and_then(|s| s.to_str()) == Some("jsonl") {
185                  if let Ok(transcript) = load_session(&amp;path) {
186                      sessions.push(transcript);
187                  }
188              }
189          }
190      }
191
192      if json_mode {
193          let json_out = serde_json::to_string_pretty(&amp;sessions).into_diagnostic()?;
194          println!("{}", json_out);
195          return Ok(());
196      }
197
198      println!("\n Multi-Session Directory Scan: {}", dir.display());
199      println!(" Total Sessions Discovered: {}\n", sessions.len());
200
201      let mut table = Table::new();
202      table.load_preset(UTF8_FULL).apply_modifier(UTF8_ROUND_CORNERS).set_header(Row::from(vec![
203          "Session ID",
204          "Orchestrator",
205          "Turns",
206          "Cache Hit %",
207          "Cost USD",
208          "Savings USD",
209      ]));
210
211      for sess in &amp;sessions {
212          table.add_row(Row::from(vec![
213              Cell::new(sess.session_id.as_str()),
214              Cell::new(format!("{:?}", sess.orchestrator)),
215              Cell::new(sess.turns.len().to_string()),
216              Cell::new(format!("{:.1}%", sess.economics.cache_hit_ratio)),
217              Cell::new(format!("${:.4}", sess.economics.net_savings_usd)),
218          ]));
219      }
220
221      println!("{table}");
222      Ok(())
223  }
224
```

(File is 259 lines total.)

**Important finding — cmd_scan bug at line 217:** the "Cost USD" column header exists (line 208) but the row-building code at lines 211-219 only pushes 5 cells (Session ID, Orchestrator, Turns, Cache Hit %, Savings USD) — it's **missing the actual "Cost USD" `total_cost_usd` cell**, so the table will be misaligned/shifted by one column relative to its 6-column header. This is worth flagging to whoever plans work here.

**Critical fact for your plan:** `lumen-cli` currently has **zero** dependency on `lumen-store` (not in Cargo.toml, not imported in main.rs). There is no database wiring, no `SqliteStore::open()` call, no repository usage anywhere in the CLI. `trace`/`audit`/`scan` are pure in-memory JSONL-file commands with no persistence layer.

`AnalyticsEngine` struct, `new()`, and `process_transcript()` (lines 44-165):

```rust
 44  pub struct AnalyticsEngine {
 45      circuit_breaker: CircuitBreakerAccumulator,
 46      turn_duration: TurnDurationAccumulator,
 47      api_health: ApiHealthAccumulator,
 48      mcp_affinity: McpAffinityAccumulator,
 49      self_correction: SelfCorrectionAccumulator,
 50      context_growth: ContextGrowthAccumulator,
 51      tool_inventory: ToolInventoryAccumulator,
 52      autonomy: AutonomyAccumulator,
 53      permission_mode: PermissionModeAccumulator,
 54      artifacts: ArtifactsAccumulator,
 55      pr_link: PrLinkAccumulator,
 56      flow: FlowAccumulator,
 57      fuzzy_tools: FuzzyToolsAccumulator,
 58      stats: StatsAccumulator,
 59      schema_extractor: SchemaExtractorAccumulator,
 60      attribution: AttributionAccumulator,
 61      trajectory_dag: TrajectoryDagAccumulator,
 62      timeline: TimelineAccumulator,
 63      span_mapping: SpanMappingAccumulator,
 64  }
 65
 66  impl Default for AnalyticsEngine {
 67      fn default() -&gt; Self {
 68          Self::new()
 69      }
 70  }
 71
 72  impl AnalyticsEngine {
 73      pub fn new() -&gt; Self {
 74          Self {
 75              circuit_breaker: CircuitBreakerAccumulator::default(),
 76              turn_duration: TurnDurationAccumulator::default(),
 77              api_health: ApiHealthAccumulator::default(),
 78              mcp_affinity: McpAffinityAccumulator::default(),
 79              self_correction: SelfCorrectionAccumulator::default(),
 80              context_growth: ContextGrowthAccumulator::default(),
 81              tool_inventory: ToolInventoryAccumulator::default(),
 82              autonomy: AutonomyAccumulator::default(),
 83              permission_mode: PermissionModeAccumulator::default(),
 84              artifacts: ArtifactsAccumulator::default(),
 85              pr_link: PrLinkAccumulator::default(),
 86              flow: FlowAccumulator::default(),
 87              fuzzy_tools: FuzzyToolsAccumulator::default(),
 88              stats: StatsAccumulator::default(),
 89              schema_extractor: SchemaExtractorAccumulator::default(),
 90              attribution: AttributionAccumulator::default(),
 91              trajectory_dag: TrajectoryDagAccumulator::default(),
 92              timeline: TimelineAccumulator::default(),
 93              span_mapping: SpanMappingAccumulator::default(),
 94          }
 95      }
 96
 97      /// Runs only the attribution accumulator over a transcript's turns. `process_transcript`'s
 98      /// `by_subagent` map only ever keeps a subagent's `.attribution` field (see below), so running
 99      /// the other 18 accumulators to completion for every subagent, only to discard their output,
100      /// was pure waste -- this is the minimal equivalent computation.
101      fn attribution_only(transcript: &amp;CanonicalTranscript) -&gt; AttributionMetrics {
102          let mut attribution = AttributionAccumulator::default();
103          for turn in &amp;transcript.turns {
104              attribution.update(turn);
105          }
106          attribution.finalize()
107      }
108
109      /// Single-pass execution of all accumulators over the canonical transcript.
110      pub fn process_transcript(mut self, transcript: &amp;CanonicalTranscript) -&gt; AnalysisReport {
111          for turn in &amp;transcript.turns {
112              self.circuit_breaker.update(turn);
113              self.turn_duration.update(turn);
114              self.api_health.update(turn);
115              self.mcp_affinity.update(turn);
116              self.self_correction.update(turn);
117              self.context_growth.update(turn);
118              self.tool_inventory.update(turn);
119              self.autonomy.update(turn);
120              self.permission_mode.update(turn);
121              self.artifacts.update(turn);
122              self.pr_link.update(turn);
123              self.flow.update(turn);
124              self.fuzzy_tools.update(turn);
125              self.stats.update(turn);
126              self.schema_extractor.update(turn);
127              self.attribution.update(turn);
128              self.trajectory_dag.update(turn);
129              self.timeline.update(turn);
130              self.span_mapping.update(turn);
131          }
132
133          let mut by_subagent = BTreeMap::new();
134          for subagent in &amp;transcript.subagents {
135              let key =
136                  subagent.subagent_role.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| CompactString::from(subagent.session_id.as_str()));
137              by_subagent.insert(key, Self::attribution_only(subagent));
138          }
139
140          AnalysisReport {
141              session_id: transcript.session_id.to_string(),
142              circuit_breaker: self.circuit_breaker.finalize(),
143              turn_durations: self.turn_duration.finalize(),
144              api_health: self.api_health.finalize(),
145              mcp_affinity: self.mcp_affinity.finalize(),
146              self_corrections: self.self_correction.finalize(),
147              context_growth: self.context_growth.finalize(),
148              tool_inventory: self.tool_inventory.finalize(),
149              autonomy: self.autonomy.finalize(),
150              permission_mode: self.permission_mode.finalize(),
151              artifacts: self.artifacts.finalize(),
152              pr_link: self.pr_link.finalize(),
153              flow: self.flow.finalize(),
154              fuzzy_tools: self.fuzzy_tools.finalize(),
155              stats: self.stats.finalize(),
156              schema_extractor: self.schema_extractor.finalize(),
157              attribution: self.attribution.finalize(),
158              by_subagent,
159              trajectory_dag: self.trajectory_dag.finalize(),
160              timeline: self.timeline.finalize(),
161              otel_correlation: OtelCorrelationAccumulator::finalize(transcript),
162              span_mapping: self.span_mapping.finalize(),
163          }
164      }
165  }
```

**API surface for the CLI to consume:** `AnalyticsEngine::new()` (or `::default()`) then `.process_transcript(&amp;transcript: &amp;CanonicalTranscript) -&gt; AnalysisReport` (consumes `self` by value, single pass). This is a pure in-memory computation, no I/O, no store dependency — it takes exactly the `CanonicalTranscript` that `load_session()` in `lumen-cli/src/main.rs` already produces.

## 8. `crates/lumen-store/src/migrations.rs` — full `sessions` table schema (V2 migration, plus V7 alteration elsewhere)

The `sessions` table is defined in migration V2 (lines 48-69 of `migrations.rs`):

```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sessions (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    provider TEXT NOT NULL,
    provider_session_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    model_family TEXT NOT NULL,
    orchestrator TEXT NOT NULL,
    started_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,
    ended_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,
    wall_duration_ms INTEGER NOT NULL,
    turn_count INTEGER NOT NULL,
    cache_hit_ratio REAL NOT NULL,
    total_cost_usd REAL NOT NULL,
    baseline_cost_usd REAL NOT NULL,
    net_savings_usd REAL NOT NULL,
    efficiency_multiplier REAL NOT NULL,
    has_anomalies INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
    UNIQUE(provider, provider_session_id)
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sessions_provider_id ON sessions(provider, provider_session_id);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sessions_started_at ON sessions(started_at);
```

(No further `ALTER TABLE sessions ...` appears anywhere in the migration list — V7, the only later `ALTER TABLE`, targets `token_usage`, not `sessions`.) There are currently **7 migrations total** (`MIGRATIONS` const has 7 elements, versions 1-7):
- V1: `schema_migrations`, `ingestion_queue`, `session_snapshots`, `pipeline_runs`
- V2: `sessions`, `token_usage`
- V3: `tool_calls`, `command_events`
- V4: `findings`, `session_category_scores`
- V5: `rollups`, `discovered_categories`
- V6: unique index `ux_rollups_period` on `rollups(period_start, period_type)`
- V7: `ALTER TABLE token_usage ADD COLUMN turns INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0;`

`apply_migrations(conn: &amp;mut Connection) -&gt; Result&lt;usize, StoreError&gt;` (lines 157-194) wraps everything in a single transaction, tracks applied versions in `schema_migrations`, and idempotently skips already-applied versions.

---

### Summary of load-bearing facts for planning wiring `lumen-store` into `lumen-cli`

1. `lumen-cli` has no `lumen-store` or `rusqlite` dependency today — both would need to be added to `Cargo.toml`.
2. `SqliteStore::open(path: &amp;Utf8Path)` requires an explicit `camino::Utf8Path` — the CLI would need to decide/accept a DB path (no default constant exists in the store crate) and convert from `std::path::Path`/`PathBuf` (used throughout `main.rs`) to `Utf8Path`.
3. `AnalyticsEngine::process_transcript` is fully synchronous and already compatible with the existing `load_session() -&gt; CanonicalTranscript` flow in `main.rs` — no async runtime is present or needed.
4. `SessionFactRecord` in `models.rs` maps cleanly onto fields already available from `CanonicalTranscript`/`TokenEconomics` (`provider`, `provider_session_id`, `model_family`, `orchestrator`, `started_at`/`ended_at`, `wall_duration_ms`, `turn_count`, `economics`, `has_anomalies`) — this looks like the intended write path for persisting a scanned/traced session, but no repository method call exists in `main.rs` currently to do so; `repositories::session::SessionRepository`'s actual method signatures were not fetched (only the mod.rs re-export index) — flag if you need those next.
5. Found a pre-existing bug in `cmd_scan` (line ~211-219 of `main.rs`): the row only pushes 5 cells against a 6-column header (`"Cost USD"` column is never populated, only `"Savings USD"` appears twice conceptually but actually just shifts subsequent columns) — worth noting if this file is being modified as part of the plan.</result>

## 1. `/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/claude.rs` (472 lines, full file)

Full contents already captured above in the tool output (lines 1-473). Key bug section — **lines 356-371**:

```rust
356        let economics = TokenEconomics::calculate(
357            &amp;[TurnPricingInput {
358                usage: TurnTokenUsage {
359                    input_tokens: total_input,
360                    output_tokens: total_output,
361                    cache_creation_tokens: total_cache_creation,
362                    cache_read_tokens: total_cache_read,
363                    reasoning_tokens: 0,
364                },
365                timestamp: ended_at,
366                tier: None,
367            }],
368            &amp;model_family,
369            &amp;pricing::SEEDED,
370            last_cost_usd,
371        );
```

Confirms the bug: `total_input`/`total_output`/`total_cache_creation`/`total_cache_read` (lines 43-46) are accumulated across *every* assistant message in the whole file (lines 230-233), then wrapped in a **single synthetic `TurnPricingInput`** with `reasoning_tokens: 0` hardcoded and `timestamp: ended_at` (the session's last timestamp, not each turn's own timestamp). Per-turn `TurnTokenUsage` objects (`turn_usage`, built at lines 235-241 per assistant message) are attached to each `CanonicalTurn` (line 288: `usage: turn_usage`) but are **never** passed into `TokenEconomics::calculate` — only the pre-summed totals are. This means:
- Price-boundary-straddling sessions are priced entirely at the final turn's rate/tier.
- Reasoning tokens are always priced as 0 for Claude Code sessions even though `TurnTokenUsage` has a `reasoning_tokens` field (never populated from Claude Code JSONL — Claude's `usage` object has no reasoning field parsed here, confirming that too, at line 224-228: only `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens` read).
- `ModelTokenSummary.turns` in `economics.per_model` will report `1` (since `turns.len()` in `calculate` sees a 1-element slice), not the real per-turn count.

## 2. `/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/opencode.rs` (271 lines, full file)

Full contents captured above. This is the entirety of the file — a **JSONL-line-based** parser (`fn parse_stream&lt;'a&gt;(&amp;self, reader: Box&lt;dyn BufRead + 'a&gt;)`) that expects one-JSON-object-per-line with top-level fields `action` (`"message"`/`"run"`/`"read"`/`"edit"`/`"think"`), `source`, `args`, `thought`, `observation`, `error`, `content`, `timestamp`, and a `metrics`/`usage` object with `accumulated_cost`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`. It has the exact same single-synthetic-`TurnPricingInput` bug at lines 228-243 as claude.rs. Critically, **none of this matches real OpenCode data** — real OpenCode persists to a SQLite DB (`~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db`), not JSONL files, and the real JSON field names are completely different (see section 5 below: `tokens.input`/`tokens.output`/`tokens.reasoning`/`tokens.cache.write`/`tokens.cache.read`, `role`, `type`, `tool`, `state.input`/`state.output`, `modelID`/`providerID`, etc.) — nothing here matches. `fingerprint.rs`'s OpenCode detection (`"action":"run"`, `"observation":"`, `"action":"message"`) also does not match anything in the real DB's JSON shapes (which use `"role"`, `"type"`, `"tool"` etc., never `"action"` or `"observation"`).

## 3. `/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/crates/lumen-model/src/pricing.rs` (257 lines, full file)

Full contents captured above. `seed()` (lines 48-150) seeds exactly these models, each with Input/CacheWrite/CacheRead/Output rows plus a Reasoning row (added in the loop at lines 134-147, copied from each model's own Output rate):

- `claude-3-5-sonnet`: Input 3.00, CacheWrite 3.75, CacheRead 0.30, Output 15.00, Reasoning 15.00
- `claude-3-5-haiku`: Input 0.80, CacheWrite 1.00, CacheRead 0.08, Output 4.00, Reasoning 4.00
- `qwen-2.5-coder`: Input 0.20, CacheRead 0.05, Output 0.60, Reasoning 0.60 (no CacheWrite row)
- `claude-opus`: Input 15.00, CacheWrite 18.75, CacheRead 1.50, Output 75.00, Reasoning 75.00
- `gpt-4o`: Input 2.50, CacheWrite 2.50, CacheRead 1.25, Output 10.00, Reasoning 10.00
- `deepseek-r1`: Input 0.55, CacheWrite 0.55, CacheRead 0.14, Output 2.19, Reasoning 2.19
- `kimi-k1.5`: Input 0.50, CacheWrite 0.50, CacheRead 0.10, Output 2.00, Reasoning 2.00
- `glm-4-plus`: Input 1.40, CacheWrite 1.40, CacheRead 0.20, Output 1.40, Reasoning 1.40
- `gemini-2.0-flash`: Input 0.10, CacheWrite 0.10, CacheRead 0.025, Output 0.40, Reasoning 0.40
- `gemini-2.0-pro`: Input 1.25, CacheWrite 1.25, CacheRead 0.30, Output 5.00, Reasoning 5.00

**No `gpt-5`/`gpt-5.6`/OpenAI-terra family row exists** — so any real OpenCode session using `gpt-5.6-terra-fast` (as seen in the live DB, section below) will fail `normalize_model_key` and fall back to `claude-3-5-sonnet`'s rate per the CRIT-LUMEN-008 fallback (line 239: `let lookup_model = normalized.unwrap_or("claude-3-5-sonnet");`).

`normalize_model_key` (lines 165-180) splits the raw model string on `-`, and from longest to shortest prefix, tests whether every stripped trailing segment is "safe" via `is_safe_strip_segment` (lines 199-214: pure-numeric segments, `digits+singleletter` size markers like `70b`, or the literal allowlist `"exp"|"latest"|"preview"|"instruct"|"chat"`); the first prefix that both passes that safety check AND exists in `self.rates` is returned. `rate_for` (lines 237-256) then looks up `(lookup_model, tier, kind, as_of)`, falling back from a requested tier to `tier: None` if no exact-tier row exists, and returns `0.0` if the recognized model has no row at all for that kind (never substituting another model's rate).

## 4. `/Users/gabe/Projects/lumen/crates/lumen-model/src/economics.rs` (144 lines, full file)

Full contents captured above.

`TurnPricingInput` (lines 13-17):
```rust
pub struct TurnPricingInput {
    pub usage: TurnTokenUsage,
    pub timestamp: DateTime&lt;Utc&gt;,
    pub tier: Option&lt;CompactString&gt;,
}
```

`TokenEconomics::calculate` signature (lines 56-61):
```rust
pub fn calculate(
    turns: &amp;[TurnPricingInput],
    model_name: &amp;str,
    pricing: &amp;PricingTable,
    provided_cost_usd: Option&lt;f64&gt;,
) -&gt; Self
```

The function is already correctly written to be per-turn (loops `for turn in turns` at line 70, looks up rates at `turn.timestamp`/`turn.tier` per iteration, sums `turn_cost` into `total_cost_usd`). The doc comment (lines 50-55) explicitly states the intended contract: "Computes full economic summary from one `TurnPricingInput` per turn... required so a session whose turns straddle a price-change boundary is priced correctly." Both `claude.rs` and `opencode.rs` violate this contract by passing a single-element slice with pre-summed totals instead of one `TurnPricingInput` per real turn.

`ModelTokenSummary.turns` is set from `turns.len() as u64` (line 122) — confirming that with the current one-synthetic-turn call pattern, this field is always `1` for every session regardless of actual turn count.

`event` is an append-only event-sourcing log that mirrors `session`/`message`/`part` state changes wrapped in `{sessionID, info/part, time}` envelopes with dotted `type` names (`session.created.N`, `message.updated.N`, `message.part.updated.N`) — redundant with, not a replacement for, the `message`/`part` tables.

### Table counts (this local DB):
```
session: 2 rows
message: 17 rows
part: 192 rows
event: 575 rows
```

### Summary of the real schema for planning purposes:
- Source of truth for parsing is **`message` JOIN `part` ON part.message_id = message.id`**, ordered by `time_created`, filtered by `session_id`.
- Real per-turn token usage/cost lives in `part` rows where `type = "step-finish"` (fields: `tokens.input`, `tokens.output`, `tokens.reasoning`, `tokens.cache.write`, `tokens.cache.read`, `cost`) — this is the correct per-turn pricing granularity, not `message.data.tokens` (which is also present per assistant message but appears to be the running message-level total, matching the last `step-finish` in that message).
- Real model identity is `message.data.modelID` + `message.data.providerID` (assistant) or `message.data.model.modelID`/`.providerID` (user), e.g. `"gpt-5.6-terra-fast"` / `"openai"` — **not** in `pricing.rs`'s seeded table at all, so it currently falls back to `claude-3-5-sonnet` pricing via `normalize_model_key`'s `None` fallback.
- Tool calls/results live in `part` rows with `type = "tool"`: `tool` (name), `callID`, `state.input`, `state.output`, `state.status`, `state.time.start/end`.
- None of `crates/lumen-session/src/adapters/opencode.rs`'s current field expectations (`action`, `source`, `args`, `thought`, `observation`, `metrics`/`usage.accumulated_cost`) exist anywhere in this real schema — the entire parsing strategy (JSONL line-by-line) is wrong; it needs to become a SQLite-reading adapter against `message`+`part` (and likely `session` for the top-level model/title/directory), and `fingerprint.rs`'s OpenCode detection branch (lines 29-35, keyed on JSONL string markers) will also need to change to detect a SQLite file (e.g. by path/extension or SQLite header magic bytes `SQLite format 3\0`) rather than a byte-sample JSON string scan.</result>
